# CORTHEX-MEMORY: test plan

[한국어](memory.ko.md)

Stage 12 of CORTHEX's own project, for the `corthex-memory` plugin. Status: confirmed on 2026-10-27; the retained Git sync design is superseded by D3. C1 0.2.0 was released on 2026-11-04; the recorded results below cover only the cases marked as passing. This stage's checkpoint is the release decision, go or no-go, which comes once for each release step: 0.2.0 in TASK-030 after TASK-028, TASK-057 and the checks/reviews, 0.3.0 after the agent-run short C2 sample evaluation (TASK-050) and 0.4.0 under the replacement D3 design and C3 trial decision (TASK-055 retains the superseded Git plan); [The release decision](#the-release-decision) says what each one reads. When this file and its Korean translation differ, this file is the source. The 2026-11-03 C2 additions and I-06/I-07 test notes add TC-339 to TC-394 as not run. C1 verdicts and removed history remain unchanged.

Inputs: the user flows in [docs/03-user-flow/memory/](../03-user-flow/memory/README.md) (FL-001 to FL-012 and FL-117), the acceptance criteria of the functional spec in [docs/04-functional-spec/memory.md](../04-functional-spec/memory.md) (SPEC-001 to SPEC-039 and SPEC-143 to SPEC-145), the memory store in [docs/05-erd/memory.md](../05-erd/memory.md), the interfaces and their codes in [docs/06-api/memory.md](../06-api/memory.md), the tasks and the outcomes that each one tests in [docs/10-wbs/memory.md](../10-wbs/memory.md), and the non-functional requirements of the [requirements](../02-prd/memory.md); above all the notes of stages 03 to 06 for this stage, which [Notes of earlier stages](#notes-of-earlier-stages) maps to cases. The memory plugin has no screens, so no case names a screen state (SCR): the memory skill's answers in the conversation take their place, and the kind of cases about screen states does not apply. The maintainer confirmed D1, D2, D4 and the seven requirement changes on 2026-10-27; D3 chose the HTTPS gateway and server in stage 02. The retained Git sync details have status `superseded (D3, 2026-10-27)` and need a replacement design before C3.

> D3 design status: `superseded (D3, 2026-10-27)`. The Git sync proposal retained here was replaced by the confirmed D3 decision on 2026-10-27. It is historical material, not an implementation or execution plan. The HTTPS gateway and server sync design will be specified separately before C3. Local files and the SQLite search index remain the device store. This notice also covers sync-only parts of shared rows, configuration and examples; their C1/C2 parts remain confirmed.

## How to read this file

- Each test case (TC) is one check with its expected result. New cases take the next unused number, stay in their release step, and are never renumbered or deleted: a case that no longer applies keeps its row, with the last result `removed (YYYY-MM-DD)` and the reason.
- `From`: what the case checks. A flow (FL); an acceptance criterion, as `SPEC-002#1` for the first of SPEC-002 in the functional spec's order, or a behaviour as a whole, as `SPEC-002`; an outcome of an interface, as `API-002#lock-busy`, with the codes of [stage 10's outcomes](../10-wbs/memory.md#outcomes); `commands#…`, `model-calls#…` and `failure-log#…` for the codes that every command, every model call, and the hooks and the memory worker in the failure log can have, the last three rows of that table; a non-functional requirement (REQ); and the records (TBL) whose fields the case checks. Every case names a flow, an acceptance criterion, a behaviour or a requirement.
- `Task`: the task of stage 10 whose pull request writes the case and makes it pass; a case that several tasks share lists them all, and the first of them writes it. Every acceptance criterion has a case for each task whose `Done when` names it, and every outcome has a case for each task that tests it.
- `Runs as`: `contract`, a check of a schema, a fixed text or a release file; `api`, a test in `tests/` that runs the hook script, the memory worker or `memory.py` with a temporary memory store, as a host or the memory skill would; `e2e`, the hook checks in CI, which run the hooks in Claude Code and Codex on Linux, macOS and Windows; `agent`, host agents walking a flow ([Walking the flows](#walking-the-flows)); `manual`, the maintainer. `real` marks a case in which memory itself calls a real model API or a real Git host, with the maintainer's keys or account and at a cost; such cases never run in CI. The host's own model, which every `agent` case uses, does not count.
- `Expected`: the outcome and its effect on the memory store: the code of stage 06, or what is shown or printed, and what changed, or that nothing did. Unless a case says otherwise, every hook exits with 0 and leaves the host session as it was, and no code, error text or key reaches the conversation.
- `Last result`: `not run`, or the result with where it ran and the commit, such as `pass on Linux, macOS and Windows in CI (commit abc1234)`; a performance result also gives its percentile and its load. `superseded (D3, 2026-10-27)` is a design status for historical C3 cases and never a passing or blocked execution result. A pass against a test server shows that memory handles the answer it was given, not that the provider gives it.

## Where the cases run

Never on a user's memory store, and on the maintainer's only where a case says so:

- Unless a case says otherwise, `api` cases run in CI on Python 3.8 and on the newest Python, on Linux, macOS and Windows (TASK-028), and on a developer's device before a pull request.
- Each test gets a new memory store, named by `CORTHEX_MEMORY_HOME`, and a temporary home folder with its own project list, so no case reads or changes a real memory store, project list or host setting.
- Model calls go to test servers on `127.0.0.1`, named by `CORTHEX_MEMORY_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `CORTHEX_MEMORY_OPENAI_BASE_URL`, which give recorded answers. The memory keys are made up while the test runs, and none is written in the repository. Git runs against local bare repositories, or as a stand-in for `git` that answers as each known host does.
- The `e2e` cases are the hook checks of `.github/hook-check/`, which TASK-028 and TASK-099 extend: they install the plugin in Claude Code and Codex, at the versions that the check workflow pins, on Linux, macOS and Windows, with a mock model server, made-up keys and a demo project.
- The fixtures are the examples of [memory-examples.json](../06-api/memory-examples.json), as stage 06 asks, and made-up conversations. No case reads a real conversation but the maintainer's own, on the maintainer's device: the trials and measurements of TASK-046 to TASK-048, TASK-050 and TASK-055.
- Cases name roles, not accounts: the user, the agent (the host's model in the session), the maintainer, another device of the same user, and a temporary device.

- Keep TASK-066 and TASK-067 helper evidence separate from actual session starts. TASK-069 and TASK-017 check TC-059–TC-065's worker/session-start steps, and TASK-021 checks TC-061's status display of lock skips. Do not promote partial evidence to a whole-case pass before those integrations.
- TC-338 tests only the detached-launch adapter with a fixture child. Real worker maintenance/lifetime remains TASK-069 and actual session-start integration TASK-017; no direct fixture result promotes TC-056, TC-057, TC-331 or TC-332 to a whole-case pass.

## C1: recording and the start summary

Release 0.2.0 has 138 cases, TC-001 to TC-115 and TC-316 to TC-338, used by TASK-001 to TASK-030, TASK-057 and TASK-060 to TASK-069. TC-114 and TC-319 remain removed history, as do TASK-015, TASK-016, TASK-018, TASK-029 and TASK-062. The 138 retained C1 rows include these two removed cases, leaving 136 active cases.

The Windows hook check records the app execution alias metadata for the current user's WindowsApps bash.exe. The no-Git-Bash variants permit the Microsoft WSL package alias to wsl.exe and the existing System32 launcher. Other bash.exe and sh.exe candidates still fail the shell check. [The runner image that exposed the alias](https://github.com/kodonghui/corthex/actions/runs/42180746169/job/127769520982).

TC-336 follow-up: on [Windows Python 3.15](https://github.com/kodonghui/corthex/actions/runs/42333315054/job/128284590965), `test_tc336_interruption_after_deletion_cannot_count_reused_seq_as_processed` appended seq 4 where 1 was expected. The rewrite's first raw acquisition had used nearly all of the shared 50 ms wait and the second lease's own checks used the rest, so the free raw lock was refused with `LockBusy` before any attempt. The method's `RuntimeError` check accepted that as its injected stop. Each acquisition now makes a first attempt and waits only within the shared budget. A new direct method replays that timing, and the two interruption methods require their injected stop. On Linux Python 3.13 the same timing reproduced the seq-4 failure before the change and passes after it. Earlier verdicts are unchanged.

The [recording contention follow-up](runs/2026-11-04-memory-capture-contention.md) supplements TC-336/TC-337 and TASK-030 without changing earlier case verdicts. The stress adds an expired seed and verifies an actual inode replacement on every success, retaining 100 successes, 500 attempts, 120 seconds and the 10ms writer interval. Recording wait remains 50ms. Historical #101/#113 observations are not closed by a fresh pass.

On/off integration follow-up #54: the real default registry selects the on/off handlers. The locked header pin uses the bytes accepted by the typed parser, the effect carries the common gate's original root fence, and locked store/configuration/curation recovery notices flush through bound diagnostics only after data leases end. Before these three fixes, the three added methods produced 11 failing subcases on each Windows interpreter; afterward the isolated combined fixture ran all 21 enablement methods with 20 passes and one host file-symlink-permission skip on each. Those fixture results are separate from this final combined candidate's required full/CI/review gates. Its disposable fixture root is canonicalized, following the shared-command macOS path correction, so handler probes use the same root as the production fence. The original interrupted v4 and older 740/742/743 attempts remain historical artifacts, not proof of this candidate. A subsequent independent review found that store/curation/layer release reports were omitted. The actual on/off regression first failed four subcases on each Windows interpreter; the fixed focused suite ran 22 methods, with 21 passes and one file-symlink-permission skip on each. Release notices are queued until all data leases end, include only the final exception class, preserve completed results, and cannot write after a changed device. The superseded v5 full run was cancelled without a pass claim; its output and cancellation receipt are retained. Only TC-079–085 and TASK-019/020 are promoted; all other case results and task states are preserved. No real user store, provider request, native-host walk, human trial or percentile claim is included. Frozen implementation SHA256: enablement.py 02160af6984ad9614b3e4fb9d88b8c3715eafd343097f8e881defc11175347ab; test_memory_enablement.py 2748acb9959710c6e296f56f9cc8150b06a45f4be083616190c9ac26be46a555.

Review-fix evidence #50: the CODE review reproduced a real P2: a summary error set the gate-error flag and suppressed required maintenance after safe output. The same regression failed before the fix and passed afterward; output-failure and blocked-store/configuration checks also passed. FINAL separates presentation failures from earlier gate/record errors, keeps the original device/deadline and lets the worker recheck the store. The interrupted FINAL-v1 whole-suite attempt is retained as interrupted, not passed; amended FINAL matrices supply the current-source proof.

Retention contention regression follow-up #46: the original 100 successes, 500 attempts, 120 seconds and 10-ms appender remain unchanged. Earlier failures and discarded candidates are retained in the PR; the metadata-instrumented pass is separate from the final uninstrumented full checks. Exact CODE `51727156e34d` ran 629 tests per Python on Linux and Windows with zero failures. Only these two direct-helper Last results change; the other 335 results, 58 passing C1 cases and existing coverage remain unchanged.

Retention performance follow-up for issue #62: raw chunk reads now use one path-and-authority guard at each read boundary; the backup probe supplies its own candidate-path guard. Retry/open, FD/name identity, resumed reads, header byte digests, current lease tokens and durable moves remain checked. Descendant path checks avoid one unused root stat while a check of the root itself keeps its original metadata read. With AC observed before and after each uninstrumented run, the original 100-rewrite stress took 81.684s then 63.672s on Windows Python 3.8.20, and 47.204s then 40.100s on 3.15.2. Candidate attempts were 101/100, retention skips 1/0, and every acknowledged append survived. This is one baseline/candidate pair per interpreter, not a general speed or reliability guarantee; the earlier 74/100 battery-run failure remains recorded separately. The original test bytes and 100/500/120-second/10-ms limits are unchanged. The 36 focused rewrite/header-fence methods, including backup metadata lease loss and lease loss after a yielded raw chunk, passed without skips on both interpreters. Only the existing TC-337 evidence is extended; all other last-result cells and task states remain unchanged, with C1 still at 24/37 tasks and 80/138 passing cases.

Command recovery follow-up (TASK-065, TC-077): shared store/configuration gates queue actual backup-restoration notices until their read leases are released. Best-effort restore and failure logging retains the original root fence and validated device, refusing a replacement root or a foreign-device backup. Three added regression methods reproduced missing restore notices and foreign-backup adoption on the earlier main source; the negative replacement case already passed. The initial 22-method command suite passed on Windows Python 3.8.20 (40.714s) and 3.15.2 (34.286s), without skips, before the separate retention performance prerequisite. The first macOS CI attempt at 89cb70fafcb3 failed two effect-lock fixtures that supplied raw temporary paths instead of the canonical store folder. A disposable Windows directory junction reproduced both failures; resolving the fixture root made both pass while the same production fence still rejected aliased lock paths without changing store bytes or timestamps. Only the fixture root is canonicalized; production path checks and the original retention stress are unchanged. The combined candidate keeps that reviewed recovery code and the completed performance dependency; its full-check results are recorded in the PR. Raw earlier failures remain preserved. This is shared command-framework evidence only: on/off effects are not added, no new task or case is marked done, and C1 stays at 24/37 tasks and 80/138 passing cases. The historical command-runner hashes above refer to that earlier candidate.

| TC-174 | A forget while a curate request waits | SPEC-012, API-005#stale, API-005#ok | TASK-088 | api | Forget an item of the project while a curate request waits for its answer | The results are dropped as `stale` and the attempt is logged with `stale`; the request built again carries `[forgotten]` instead of the forgotten words, and its answer applies, with no forgotten text in any item or line and no failure counted; the forget command itself is TASK-097 and TASK-098; follow-up (commit 3a7a9e41271e): a forget while an attempt waits for an answer that then fails, while one part of a turn in parts waits, or while a request is counted sends nothing more of that attempt, neither the retry nor the other parts nor the counted request: the next request to go is the one built again, without the forgotten words, and a failed attempt whose fence did not move is still tried again with the same request; second follow-up (commit c735efa9b81f): turning the project off while one part of a turn in parts waits or while a request is counted, and withdrawing consent before a failed request is tried again, send nothing more of that attempt: it is stale rather than a failure, nothing applies, and the run ends as `disabled` or `consent`; third follow-up (commit 62248ca8872d): the same when consent is withdrawn while the retry waits for the layer lease, and a run whose consent went before a retry ends as `consent` without waiting for the lease; fourth follow-up (commit 9d7674587abb): when the conditions read as gone once before the retry and hold again right after, the attempt is stale, not failed, and the run sends the request built again; when that reading fails with an error, the failed attempt counts its request as sent |

## C3: sync

D3 design status: `superseded (D3, 2026-10-27)`. The 37 cases below retain the earlier Git contract for traceability. They are not executable until replaced by the separately confirmed server design before C3.

The 37 cases of 0.4.0, TC-279 to TC-315, written by TASK-052 to TASK-056 and TASK-059.

## Performance

Each performance requirement is measured as it is written, with its percentile, its load and where it ran. Results from a container or a developer's device are reported apart from those of the CI runners and of the maintainer's devices, and a claim that a change made something faster needs runs before and after it under the same conditions.

| Requirement | What it asks | Cases | Measured | Load | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REQ-011 | Capture hooks within 100 ms at the 95th percentile | TC-105, TC-114, TC-274 | Each whole capture hook, timed from outside, the interpreter's start included: the median and the 95th percentile | 100 runs of each hook in each host, with a memory store of 1,000 entries; in the trials, the maintainer's usual work | The CI runners of Linux, macOS and Windows; the maintainer's devices |
| REQ-034 | Prompt hook within 1 second at the 95th percentile | TC-256, TC-207, TC-274 | The whole prompt hook with recall, timed from outside: the median and the 95th percentile | 100 runs in each host with 1,000, 5,000 and 20,000 items and a test server that answers at once; an embedding 2 seconds late; the trial's prompts | The CI runners of the three systems; a developer's device; the maintainer's device in the trial of C2 |

Redaction's time, which stage 06 measured only on Linux, is measured on the three systems by TC-028; it is not a requirement, but it counts toward the 100 ms of REQ-011.

## Walking the flows

The stage's browser agents walk screens. The memory plugin has none, so host agents walk its flows instead: sessions of Claude Code and Codex, each with the plugin installed in a temporary home, a made-up project and a memory store of its own, against the test servers, driven by an agent that plays the user from the run list below and then reads the raw logs, the memory store and the memory skill's answers. They run on a developer's device or in a cloud session, never in CI, and only with made-up conversations. The walks are TASK-057 for C1, TASK-101 and TASK-102 for C2 and TASK-059 for C3.

- When: before each trial, after a large change, when the maintainer asks, and again on the release's commit before its tag.
- What: every path of every flow in the table below, in both hosts, in Korean and in English. The run list of a walk is these rows.
- Report: one file per run in `docs/12-qa/runs/`, named `<date>-<where>.md`, with the commit, the hosts and their versions, and for each row of the run list a verdict, `works`, `fails` or `blocked`, with its evidence: the prompt sent, what the agent answered, and what the raw log, the memory store or the memory status held. Impressions, such as an answer that was hard to follow, are listed apart from the verdicts; the maintainer decides which become bugs, tasks or changes to the requirements.
- After a run: each `fails` is filed as a bug ([Bugs](#bugs)), and each row that works becomes a replay in the hook checks, so that CI runs it on every pull request.

## The release decision

Record each release decision with the evidence below. On 2026-11-04 the maintainer removed the C1 one-week trial and preauthorized tagging 0.2.0 immediately after passing checks. TASK-030 records go/no, tags without asking again and notifies the maintainer. Only new recording loss in TASK-057 requires one question before the tag; continue the next development while waiting:

1. The last result of every case of the step and of the steps before it, on the release's commit: the `api` and `e2e` cases in CI, the `contract` cases with the release checks, and the walk of every flow on that commit.
2. The open bugs, and each case not run, with the reason.
3. For 0.2.0, retain TASK-028 timings/failures and TASK-057 walks, plus one line each for #95, #98, #101, #105, #106 and #107 in release notes and go. TASK-029 and TC-114 remain removed history, never an executed trial. Real conversation samples are approved; on 2026-11-05 TASK-050 became an agent-run short sample evaluation. TASK-055 retains the superseded Git plan; C3 trial policy and replacement tasks follow the server design.
4. The cost of the `real` cases and of the trial, from the usage log.

A no-go names what must change. A go is followed by the release task, TASK-030, TASK-051 or TASK-056, which tags the commit that passed.

Under the 2026-11-05 delegation the agent runs TASK-050 as a short sample evaluation and classifies repeated explanations as not saved (no item), not retrieved (item exists but was not injected), or not used (injected but not followed). Record temporal coverage and unmeasured limits instead of inferring a live-duration trial. Optional 4/9-week checks remain off; C3 evaluation is specified with its server design.

## Bugs

The store-identity failure is tracked in [issue #39](https://github.com/kodonghui/corthex/issues/39), with TC-327 as its deterministic regression. Each bug is an issue of the repository, titled with its BUG ID, in this form, and the last result of its case links it:

```markdown
## BUG-NNN What fails, in a few words

From: TC-NNN · the flow, acceptance criterion and outcome it breaks
Steps: ...
Expected: ...
Actual: ...
Environment: the system, the host and its version, Python, and the commit
Trace ID: the outcome that went wrong, such as `API-003#ok`
Evidence: ...
```

## Choices this stage made

Each can change at the checkpoint.

1. One file for the memory plugin's plan, `memory.md`, like the files of the other stages, instead of the stage's default name `test-plan.md`. The reports of the walks go in `docs/12-qa/runs/`, the default.
2. A `Task` column, which the stage's template does not have, so that each case has an owner and this plan and stage 10's `Done when` say the same.
3. Host agents in Claude Code and Codex in place of browser agents, since the memory plugin has no screens; the rows that work become replays in the hook checks in place of end-to-end tests of screens.
4. The `api` tests in `tests/`, next to the tests of 0.1.0, instead of `tests/api/`; the `e2e` tests are the hook checks in `.github/hook-check/`.
5. The mark `real` for the cases in which memory calls a real API or a real Git host, which cost money and whose answers vary, so that they never run in CI.
6. This file as the truth of the stage, like the other stages of this project: a case's last result changes here, in the pull request that ran it, and the Notion copy follows.

A shared case needs every named slice and its integration before a whole-case pass; library/replay evidence is recorded as partial. C2 uses the agent-run short real-sample evaluation approved on 2026-11-05; C3 evaluation is set with the D3 server design. TASK-050 remains, and optional 4/9-week checks are not enabled.

Passed on 2026-10-27: the recommendations listed below, with 100-day default retention and the new D3 decision recorded in stage 02. Git-specific recommendations are superseded, and the server sync design is deferred until before C3. This confirms the plan and choices; C1 follows the 2026-11-04 automatic-tag approval after checks and walks, while the split and real samples are approved and C2 uses the agent-run short real-sample evaluation approved on 2026-11-05; C3 evaluation is set with the D3 server design.

Under the 2026-11-04 approval, TASK-030 records the first release decision, of 0.2.0, after TASK-028, TASK-057 and the checks/reviews. Before that, most worth a look:

## Notes for later stages

### Stage 11: development

- Each pull request writes the cases of the tasks it finishes, with the case's ID in each test's name or docstring, and sets their last results here to where they ran and the commit. A case found wrong is changed here first, in the same pull request.
- A case that its task cannot pass as written is not dropped: its last result says why, and the maintainer decides.
- Before C2 and before C3, when stage 10 splits their tasks, each new task takes over the cases of the part it builds, and the cases keep their IDs.

2026-11-05 decision: the maintainer chose a short C2 real-conversation sample evaluation instead of a one-week trial and delegated the tests to the agent. The agent selects and locally redacts representative samples, records automatic measurements separately from agent quality judgements, and does not label those judgements as human acceptance. TASK-048 retains at least 30 judged prompts. TASK-050 reports all seven measures after implementation/verification, with observed scope, sample size, costs and explicit unmeasured limits; a short replay is not proof of a week of live use or a measured month of billing. Existing quality targets remain: an unobserved time-based target is recorded as unmeasured, never silently passed. C3 evaluation is set with the D3 server design, while TASK-055 remains superseded Git history. The agent prepares go/no evidence and a recommendation; the existing release decision/tag authorization remains separate. Optional 4/9-week checks are not enabled by this decision.

TASK-074 private identity/intent component: read-only Windows 64-bit volume/128-bit file IDs and POSIX opened-node IDs, sticky process bindings, exact original metadata and deterministic pinned-epoch regeneration are implemented without a journal writer. The codec accepts cold strict C1 metadata only; missing progress and named migration headers cannot create a new cold intent. TC-390 remains partial/not run as a whole: durable journal/checkpoints, interruption/resume effects, original host coordination, initialization/lineage and current privacy-fact checks are not supplied by these components. Full cross-platform unit/strict qualification is recorded at the reviewed commit; no live store migration or provider request is performed.

TASK-079 curator client component adds versioned instructions, complete-field XML redaction, a closed request builder, one original 30-minute retry budget and per-attempt TBL-003 accounting. Normalized retry-after survives stalled error bodies. Endpoint/auth/config snapshots remain original across retries while fresh environment values are re-redacted. A separate one-second usage-log cleanup window grants no network/apply extension, and cancellation preserves the original ownership exception without inventing a provider error. Synthetic loopback and controlled-time fixtures do not qualify actual-provider behavior, quote/provenance/application, rejected-key presentation or whole TC-122/123/124/128/130 outcomes. TASK-079 stays in progress; no hook activation, live migration, key request or release tag is performed.

Windows Python 3.8 functional fixtures twice reached their shortened pre-header timeout in different cases; the initial logs are retained and the exact cause is unconfirmed. The proxy case passed three isolated unchanged-source runs. Functional fixtures now allow the normal 30-second connect/whole fixture budget, rather than an unrelated three-second startup/connect budget; explicit byte-gap/whole/startup/deadline tests retain their short injected limits. Production 30/600/1800-second limits are unchanged. Earlier frozen v1/v2 results qualify those snapshots only.

A bounded unpublished-client probe found that NUL-interleaved UTF-16 text could bypass the literal DTD/declaration guard and expand a fixture entity. Reject NUL before the XML parser, keeping XML Unicode input unambiguous; UTF-16/32 carrier regression fixtures accompany the change. No live request or released installation used this draft. All final bytes are qualified again; earlier v3 results do not qualify the fix.

TASK-074 cold preservation component creates exclusive ordinal originals, immutable plan and a final sealed preparation record outside recovery. It retains original metadata/directory native identities and checks original ownership, exact bytes and namespace membership throughout guarded writes, file sync/close and POSIX directory barriers. Failed/short writes, every regular-file sync boundary, close uncertainty, changed artifacts, extra links and lost ownership keep canonical headers/progress unchanged and preserve partial auxiliary data. Raw conversations are not read or copied. Cold checks and separate artifact-only checks grant no initialization, resume or history-repair authority. TASK-074/TC-390 remain in progress/partial until exact publication-prefix checkpoints, interrupted/resumed effects, finalization and current privacy/lineage gates are connected; these fixtures do not prove physical power-failure behavior.

The TASK-074 header-only publisher fixes exact native stage IDs before effects, evacuates preserved header temporaries, publishes backup before primary and writes exclusive hash-linked checkpoints. Source bytes/namespace/native IDs and the original owner remain bound; a final callback-free fact check catches changes made during the last owner callback. Direct filesystem fixtures cover exact order, old C1 restoration with a missing/damaged primary, the known hardlink decrement, failures before/after rename, sync/checkpoint failure, changed payloads and sticky revocation. No C2 project data or completed header is published. Interrupted reopening, project initialization, completed-store finalization and privacy/lineage recovery remain TASK-074; TC-390 stays partial/not run as a whole.

Header checkpoints are staged under fresh exclusive private names, synced/closed and renamed before their fixed primary becomes visible. Short checkpoint writes leave only preserved unpublished staging; uncertain auxiliary rename never initiates a canonical header effect. Old frozen-draft results do not qualify this final source. No header is completed or C2 project initialized by the header-only port.

The TASK-074 private header-resume port reacquires an actual fresh maintenance owner, reads only fixed metadata families, verifies exact sealed originals/root/native IDs and binds a current named migrating header to its recorded native stage. Verified effects and canonical hash-linked checkpoints determine the exact remaining prefix; copied headers, wrong plans, damaged/boolean-version checkpoints, unknown auxiliary records and post-initialization progress refuse. Partial unpublished checkpoint staging stays preserved. Interrupted backup/primary effects reuse the original planned bytes/stage IDs and write no raw/project data or completed headers. A renewed raw scope is actually leased afresh; it is not evidence of the earlier interval. TASK-074/TC-390 remain in progress/partial until project initialization, completed-header finalization, actual host coordination and privacy/lineage recovery are connected.

The full-unit CI job limit is45 minutes. The actual #143 Windows3.8 suite took1376.813 seconds for1483 cases before the new preservation/header/resume filesystem fixtures; their measured focused runs add enough time to risk the old30-minute job limit including setup. This changes no test selection, failure requirement or product deadline, and is not a performance pass. The final combined source still needs full unit/strict checks before commit and current-head platform CI/reviews.

Resuming any verified header prefix re-flushes both canonical and private journal directories before reconstructing a checkpoint or returning an existing completed fence. Visibility and a journal checkpoint alone cannot substitute for the missed original rename barriers. If a returned prefix check later fails, it revokes the original maintenance observer together with its own prefix/guard; repairing the fixture cannot revive that same interval. PR146 review findings are reproduced by fault fixtures, not waived. These barriers still rely on the documented OS/filesystem/storage guarantees and are not physical power-failure attestation.

The TASK-074 migration-owned initialization component requires the actual original C1-lineage coordinator and the exact fully migrating live header fence. It stages all file/empty-directory native IDs before effects, evacuates preserved progress temps, publishes fixed-epoch progress backup before primary, initializes empty C2 state/removed/pins/list/history/conflicts and writes a final protocol-bound initialization fact only after exact verified effects. Unknown preexisting C2 records, even empty backups, refuse. Raw data is never read/copied. Directory/file uncertainty, lost original lineage, final-callback source drift and missing completion facts preserve partial effects without replay or activation. An initial unfrozen nested-check prototype hit the original120-second fixture deadline; the complete original owner/lineage gates now precede callback-free artifact/native/byte/stamp checks, and the same-budget probe passed. Neither that probe nor these fixtures establishes product performance, real host lineage, physical power durability or history-recovery permission. Initialization resumption, completed-header finalization and current privacy/lineage recovery remain TASK-074; TC-390 stays partial/not run.

The reviewed header-resume rename barriers and interval revocation fixes are included in this combined initialization source. Initialization's original lineage-bound returned checks now revoke the original owner too; auxiliary repairs cannot revive the interval. There are12 component fixtures, including returned-scope revocation. All earlier source results qualify their own bytes only. This source requires full/strict checks before commit and current-head platform CI/reviews. Completed-header finalization, initialization resumption, actual driver/preflight and current privacy/lineage recovery remain TASK-074; TC-390 remains partial/not run.

TASK-074 initialization resumption verifies the original sealed archive, header fence, fixed initialization manifest and the exact native file/directory publication prefix under a renewed actual owner and original None-only C1-lineage coordinator. It keeps the original intent, round, epoch and raw bytes; only a verified effect can supply its next missing checkpoint. An entirely unpublished orphan round and partial checkpoint staging are preserved before a new unpublished round is built inside the same migration intent. A named malformed manifest is never treated as absence. Unknown items are not read/healed/adopted; copied records, serialized paths, boolean protocol versions and completed headers refuse. Completed initialization is verified without republication. Original callback exceptions retain identity and revoke the scope. Directory membership/native identity and all read file bindings remain pinned through handoff. Fixtures do not establish real host lineage or supported history recovery. TASK-074 remains in progress and TC-390 partial/not run; completed-header finalization, actual driver/preflight and current privacy/non-deletion lineage recovery remain.

The combined initialization-resume source includes reviewed rename-directory barriers and original-interval revocation across returned scopes. It flushes verified canonical effects and the private source directory before reconstructing missing checkpoints or returning existing completion;16 component cases include directory barriers, barrier failures and returned-scope revocation. Full-unit CI has60 minutes: the original thirteen initialization-resume Windows3.8 fixtures took667.594 seconds, on top of the earlier1522-case982.239-second CI and the added header/init native fixtures, risking45 minutes with setup. This changes no test selection, failure rule or product deadline and is not a performance pass. Old results qualify only old bytes. Actual driver/lineage, completed-header finalization and current privacy-safe recovery remain TASK-074; TC-390 remains partial/not run.

Initialization resumption reserves native IDs for live migrating headers and surviving metadata/auxiliary nodes; it does not permanently reserve dead named C1 header IDs from before header replacement. A controlled identity-interface case failed before the lifetime fix and passed after it. This does not claim actual physical inode reuse. The older v1 Linux full1561 run had one InvalidResume; the failing case passed one isolated traced run and three diagnosed reruns without collisions, so its exact cause remains unconfirmed. Preserve that failure and do not count older successful source runs as current qualification. The new combined source needs full1569/strict checks and current-head platform CI. Current Windows precommit focus is a six-case boundary subset plus native15/strict4 and current-source defect controls; the complete platform suite is still required before merge. TASK-074/TC-390 remain in progress/partial.

Header and project initialization resumption recheck the original native archive directories when handing the reader's evidence to the write guard. One fault fixture moves the same original files into a replacement directory in each resume path. The resumed scope refuses the replacement before publishing a checkpoint and revokes its original interval. The earlier source's review and CI evidence apply to its own commit.

macOS CI on the earlier commit exposed two barrier fixture failures because the expected project path used the temporary directory alias while the maintenance owner used the canonical path. Resolve the shared test project path before comparing the barrier calls. The same-directory identity and effect checks in the product code are unchanged from the archive-binding correction.

TASK-074 final header publication requires the exact live initialized completion chain plus original None-only C1 lineage and current-privacy checks. Both completed header stages and native IDs are durable before backup then primary publication; each effect has a canonical checkpoint and only then is migration-complete recorded. Project bytes, raw data and fixed epochs stay unchanged. Ten component fixtures exercise ordering, capability/receipt refusal, callback facts/exceptions, returned-interval revocation and failures after backup/both headers. Project bytes, raw data and fixed epochs stay unchanged. The TASK-074 migration driver adds six TC-390 tests: classification of every named-header and record state with refusals before any lease, one fresh synthetic migration that a repeat call leaves without a lease, a five-step interrupted chain that resumes headers, initialization, finalization and final headers in order while an old C1 client stays refused and raw bytes stay unchanged, and a refused interval check that changes nothing. Synthetic fixture callbacks stand in for the actual coordinator; they prove no real-host interval, lineage or privacy fact. TC-390 stays partial.

TC-390 also covers the `migrate` command handler (API-154): a preview that changes nothing, a stale code after the configuration changes, a supported-release refusal, a confirmed fresh migration, a live lease answering `lock-busy` with no header change, a configuration edit during initialization that stops the run with `store-changed` and blocks the resume until the edit is undone, a confirmed resume after that, a completed store answering `nothing`, and the runner still answering `usage` for `migrate` before the 0.3.0 activation. The user's yes stands for the interval in these fixtures; no real host was closed or updated.

TASK-079 closing slice (commit 074c4b7): loopback client cases now pass TC-121, TC-123, TC-125, TC-128 and TC-133, and the status reader shows a set key as `rejected` after a 401 until a later call of that API. They remain synthetic: actual-provider behaviour stays with the real calls of TASK-047 and TASK-100, the display for format-2 stores with C2 status (TASK-096), and the OpenAI halves of TC-122, TC-124, TC-130, TC-131 and TC-132 with TASK-080.

TASK-081 search index (commit fb091cf): TC-135, TC-137 and TC-138 pass on fabricated items in a Linux container with Python 3.13 and 3.8, and TC-140 records its TASK-081 part: deleting items clears their index rows, and a forget's checkpoint empties the write-ahead log and leaves none of the item's words in the file. Stage 06's word splitting now says which characters are letters, digits and marks, that a run touching a longer word of another script is not an identifier, and that each word of a request counts once. No hook or command opens the index yet; the forget command (TASK-097, TASK-098) and the vectors and their rebuild (TASK-082) remain.

TASK-082 step 6 (commit 6a9956b3c56f): TC-139, TC-140 and TC-141 pass in a Linux container with Python 3.13 and 3.8, embedding against a local test server, and TC-136 records the rebuilds and embeddings of its worker part. The step's own tests also cover the rows that it mends or deletes, an item that changes, stops being current or goes during its request, an index built again with another vector length during a request, the gates, a missing key, a lasting failure, a request cut off at the run's last 60 seconds and an index file that is a directory or a link; 26 mutants of the step and of `index_io`'s row and vector writes each fail them. After the code review of #176 (commit 59ba77a64ea0), a request is not tried again once one of its items is forgotten, changed or given a tombstone between two attempts, and is tried again when only the fence moved; a reader that keeps the log in a read transaction takes the index's files with the removed rows, and the index is built again, while it changes nothing when no row goes; an index whose files cannot go either, as a test makes them, is marked unfinished and built again at the next step 6. Of 16 mutants of these changes, 13 fail the tests; two only read a batch again or sync an index just built, which changes nothing, and one drops the lease check before the mark, which no test reaches. After the second code review (commit eaf811a3ee8c), the deleted rows take the index's `built` mark with them in their transaction, and it is put back with its value once the log is emptied: a step that stops after deleting rows, as a test stops it, leaves the index unfinished and the next step 6 builds it again, and when the log goes and the index file stays, the file, back as it was before the rows went, loses the mark again. All 11 mutants of this change fail the tests; two tests added in commit ad4468bb5d64 reach the lease checks after the log is emptied and before the mark goes again. After the third code review (commit 14925f06adbb), the step reads its conditions again before each request and each further attempt: a project turned off during the first of two requests sends no second one and ends the step as `disabled`, and consent withdrawn before a retry ends it as `consent` without the retry. All 4 mutants of this change fail the tests. After the code review of 3965d43 (commit 923c888cf111), the conditions are read after the fence comparison, which may wait for the layer lease, so that a change during that wait sends nothing more either, and a configuration that can no longer be read ends the step as `configuration`. All 5 mutants of the order fail the tests. After the code review of 13b3768 (commit c196f51e503e), they are read before that wait as well, so that a step whose conditions went during a request or before a retry ends with that reason without waiting for the lease again, and a step with no item left to embed reads them once more. All 7 mutants of these reads fail the tests. After the code review of eaef36d, stages 06 and 10 no longer say that the step ends at once: a wait for the layer lease or the run's own time can end it first, as `busy` or `deadline`, and nothing more is sent either way. No hook or command calls step 6 yet; recall and search during a rebuild (TASK-093, TASK-040) and the real calls of TASK-047 and TASK-100 remain.

TASK-080 token counts and embeddings (commit 74c958f): TC-124, TC-126, TC-127, TC-129, TC-130, TC-131 and TC-132 pass against loopback servers in a Linux container with Python 3.13 and 3.8, together with the TASK-078 and TASK-079 parts that their results name, and TC-122 records its TASK-080 part. Stage 06 now says that the count's line carries the request's instructions, that an answer with missing or repeated indexes is `bad-answer`, that failed checks keep the billed tokens, that OpenAI's `x-request-id` goes into the usage line, and that each attempt connects within 30 seconds or its shorter limit. The tests failed for each of 22 faults seeded into the new code. No hook, worker or command calls these clients yet; the real calls of TASK-047 and TASK-100 remain.
